Build full-stack Android apps with Gemini in Google AI Studio

📅 Nov 18, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Google AI Studio, with Gemini 4 Carbon and Hydrogen, now enables you to build and ship full-stack, Android applications and publish to the Play Store in minutes. You can now go from a single prompt to a production-ready Kotlin Android app complete with data persistence, user sign-in, and AI features. Simply bring your idea and the Google AI Studio Build agent takes care of the entire setup. From configuring your database to securely calling the Gemini API, you can rapidly build and test on your own device directly from the Play Store at no cost.

Alongside full-stack Firebase backend provisioning, a major wave of upgrades is rolling out across the Android development workflow in Google AI Studio. Developers can now iterate in a faster, high-definition (HD) Android emulator with real-time audio output, send one-click emulator screenshots directly to the coding agent for visual UI changes, and style apps with custom fonts.

Sorry, your browser doesn't support playback for this video

Here is what is launching today for Android app development in Google AI Studio:

Previously, Android apps generated in AI Studio were limited to local session storage. Now, when your prompt describes an Android app that needs persistent storage, user sign-in, or generative AI features, the Google AI Studio Build agent proactively detects those requirements. With your confirmation, the agent provisions the backend and wires up your Kotlin codebase automatically:

Under the hood, the Build agent automatically adds the Firebase Bill of Materials (BoM) and generates clean, idiomatic Kotlin code for your data and AI layers:

Link to Youtube Video (visible only when JS is disabled)

Building great mobile interfaces requires a tight visual and interactive feedback loop. Both the in-browser Android emulator and the Build agent's toolchain include new capabilities to help you inspect, hear, and refine your app in real time:

Sorry, your browser doesn't support playback for this video

Testing in an emulator accelerates iteration, but nothing replaces running your app on a physical device. Historically, installing a newly built Android app onto a phone required enabling USB debugging cables, managing signing keystores locally, or setting up a full developer console account.

With Google Play integrated directly into Google AI Studio, you can set up a Play account in a few clicks from the publish panel and publish your app straight to your personal Google Play library. Google AI Studio builds and signs the Android App Bundle (.aab) behind the scenes, so you can immediately download and run your custom Android app on your phone directly from Google Play.

All of these features are rolling out in Google AI Studio today. To get started, open Google AI Studio Build, select the Android chip at the top of the workspace, and try one of these prompts:

Explore the Google AI Studio documentation and Firebase Android documentation to learn more, try building your own full-stack Android app at aistudio.google.com/apps, and share what you build on X with @GoogleDevs, @GoogleAIStudio, and @AndroidDev.

Why In-Flight Wi-Fi Rarely Matches the Advertised Speed: A Plain Guide to Reading Connectivity Claims

📅 Nov 18, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Posted on Nov 18 • Originally published at flytlink.co.uk

Every in-flight connectivity announcement comes with a headline figure: a download speed, an upload speed and sometimes a latency number. These figures are rarely false. They are, however, rarely the whole story.

Flytlink covers LEO, GEO and air-to-ground (ATG) connectivity for operators, aircrew and passengers. One question sits underneath almost every story we publish: what will this actually be like on a real flight? This guide gives you the tools to answer that for yourself.

Take a business jet installation we've covered on Flytlink: Aero-Dienst fitting Starlink to a Bombardier Global 6000. The quoted figures were up to 220 Mbps download, 25 Mbps upload and latency as low as 99ms. Crucially, those numbers were described as single-user, optimal conditions.

That qualifier matters. On a business jet carrying a handful of people, the experience may come reasonably close to the headline. On a widebody airliner it's a different picture, because the same pipe is being shared by far more people.

Demonstrations often reinforce the problem. A test showing one user running several laptops proves what the system can do in ideal conditions. It doesn't show what happens when hundreds of passengers are online together, each with a phone, a tablet and a laptop.

An aircraft connection is a shared resource. Everyone on board draws from the same link between the aircraft and the satellite or ground network. The term for this sharing is contention.

Here's a worked example from our coverage. If 300 passengers on a Boeing 787 share a single satellite connection delivering somewhere between 40 and 220 Mbps, that could work out at less than 1 Mbps per passenger if everyone is active at once. That's barely enough for comfortable browsing, let alone video.

In practice not everyone connects at the same moment. But peaks do happen, often after meal service or towards the end of a long flight. Usage patterns have also changed. Passengers now expect to:

Each of these behaves differently on a congested link. Together they explain why a connection that is 'fast' on paper can feel sluggish in row 34.

Speed is how much data can move per second. Latency is how long a round trip takes. For interactive tasks such as video calls, online gaming or loading web pages made up of many small requests, latency often matters more than raw speed.

The type of network has a big influence. The table below gives a general picture of the three main approaches.

None of these is simply 'best'. As we've argued on Flytlink, GEO isn't dead; it's adapting to a multi-orbit future rather than competing head-on with LEO on latency. Meanwhile, ATG providers such as SkyFive Arabia are pursuing a ground-based approach across the Middle East.

Because contention is unavoidable, well-designed aircraft networks don't treat all traffic equally. A common approach is hierarchical quality of service (QoS), which sorts traffic into tiers:

Techniques such as Weighted Fair Queuing at the aircraft gateway router help stop one heavy user, or one type of traffic, from crowding everyone else out.

Measurement matters too. Tools such as the PEPsal Performance Dashboard, featured on Flytlink, show the latency you're actually experiencing in real time, with baseline and enhanced performance side by side. That's far more useful than a theoretical minimum.

Whether you're an operator comparing providers or a passenger deciding whether to pay for Wi-Fi, these questions cut through the marketing:

The market is moving quickly. Qatar Airways has completed Starlink installation on 54 Boeing 777 aircraft and is now working on its Airbus A350 fleet. NetJets has committed to equipping more than 600 business jets with Starlink. As more fleets switch on, the gap between headline speeds and real-world experience becomes a question that operators, crews and passengers all face.

Understanding contention, latency and traffic management won't make a congested link faster. It will help you set realistic expectations, ask better questions and choose the right service for the job.

Flytlink exists to look past best-case figures and report what in-flight connectivity actually delivers, across LEO, GEO and ATG, for operators, aircrew and passengers. For the latest analysis, news and performance insight, visit the Flytlink home page.

It’s So Easy to Turn Your Garden Into An Owl Hangout – You Just Need 5 Plants

📅 Nov 18, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Join The Club for quick access. Enter your email below and we'll send confirmation, and sign you up to our newsletter.

Beautiful plants and ideas to brighten your garden.

Expert tips for growing fruit, vegetables and herbs at home.

Want To Befriend a Crow? Here's How To Attract These Clever Birds To Your Yard and Earn Their Trust

5 Easy Fall Wins to Help Your Backyard Birds Survive Winter – How to Beat the Big Chill and Ensure More Visits

The 7 Best Native Perennials for a Garden That Gets More Beautiful Every Year

Sign up for the Gardening Know How newsletter today and receive a free copy of our e-book "How to Grow Delicious Tomatoes".

Unlock instant access to exclusive member features.

Get full access to premium articles, exclusive features and a growing list of member rewards.

Wouldn't it be magical to spot an owl in your backyard? You might be surprised how much difference the right plants can make...

When you purchase through links on our site, we may earn an affiliate commission. Here’s how it works.

There's something rather magical about spotting an owl in your backyard. These mysterious nighttime visitors can be surprisingly elusive, but there are ways to make your garden a more welcoming place for them. And no, you don't need acres of woodland or a sprawling country estate to get your very own owl hangout started.

The secret is to think beyond the owls themselves (although there's never any harm in making an owl nest box) and create a garden that supports the wildlife they depend on.

Whether you want to lure in your birth month owl, or find yourself – much like Justin Bieber – welcoming in any owl that flits by, there are plenty of ways to make your backyard more appealing to these beautiful birds of prey. And the good news? Well, you can make a surprisingly big difference with just five plants.

That's right, folks! If you want more owls in your garden, that means providing shelter for small mammals, encouraging insects and other prey, and offering trees and shrubs where owls can perch, roost or potentially nest.

From tall native grasses to berry-producing shrubs and evergreen trees, then, these are some of the best additions for creating an owl-friendly backyard.

Switchgrass (Panicum virgatum) is a fantastic native grass for an owl-friendly garden, particularly if you have a sunny spot where you can let it grow relatively undisturbed.

Why? Well, because its tall stems and dense clumps provide valuable cover for small mammals, including voles and mice, which are important prey for many owl species. The grass also offers shelter for other wildlife through winter, when more exposed parts of the garden can become less hospitable.

Sign up for the Gardening Know How newsletter today and receive a free copy of our e-book "How to Grow Delicious Tomatoes".

Switchgrass is particularly useful in meadow-style plantings, where it can grow alongside native wildflowers. It produces attractive seedheads in late summer and fall, too, and it's a relatively low-maintenance perennial once established.

Give it a sunny position and well-drained soil, then, and leave some of its growth standing over winter rather than cutting everything back in fall. You may just find it swiftly becomes your new favorite garden plant, let alone the best part of your owl hangout!

If you want to make your garden more appealing to owls, don't underestimate the value of evergreen trees! Native pines and spruces provide year-round cover, with dense branches offering potential daytime roosting spots and sheltered perches. Their evergreen foliage can also make wildlife feel more protected from disturbance and exposure.

Different owl species have different preferences, but trees are an important part of the habitat for many woodland-dwellers like barred owls. Some species also use natural cavities in mature trees for nesting.

If you have room, consider something like this stunning Eastern White Pine from Nature Hills. Just remember that a newly planted sapling is unlikely to become an instant owl retreat, though, so it's always better to preserve mature trees where you can!

A slightly wild patch of blackberry brambles (Rubus spp.) might not be everyone's idea of a perfectly manicured garden, but it's a genius addition to anyone hoping to turn their yard into an owl hangout. After all, blackberry thickets provide dense cover for small critters and nesting birds, not to mention food and shelter for plenty others besides.

Why does this matter for owls? Because a garden that supports a healthy range of small animals can help provide the prey that visiting owls need, of course! And you don't have to let blackberries take over your entire yard, either. A small patch of something like Baby Cakes® Blackberry Bush from Nature Hills along a boundary or at the back of a wildlife-friendly border can provide plenty of habitat value.

Just remember that brambles can spread, so give them room and keep growth away from paths and areas where people regularly walk.

Serviceberry (Amelanchier spp.) is a beautiful native shrub or small tree that brings several seasons of wildlife interest to the garden; think white flowers that attract pollinators, followed by edible berries in summer, which are enjoyed by birds and other wildlife.

Its value doesn't stop with the berries; serviceberry also supports insects and provides useful woody cover, helping to create a more diverse backyard habitat. For an owl-friendly garden, this is all about supporting the wider food web... but hey, it helps that it's a stunner, too!

Goldenrod (Solidago spp.) is often overlooked as a wildlife plant, but its bright yellow flowers provide an important late-season source of nectar and pollen for bees, butterflies and other insects. Those insects, in turn, help support other wildlife, including small birds and mammals.

Some smaller owl species also eat large insects, making insect-rich habitats like goldenrod a must for your owl hangout! Especially as it's a lovely way to add late-summer and fall color to your garden. Many varieties are native to North America, and once established, they're generally easy to grow.

Something like the Fireworks Solidago (Goldenrod) Plant from Burpee is a great way to go!

These five plants are a great starting point for your owl hangout, but there are plenty of other native choices that can help make your backyard more wildlife-friendly. #

Viburnums, for example, produce berries that feed birds and other wildlife, while their dense branching offers useful cover, too. Native dogwoods offer flowers, berries and dense branching that can provide shelter for wildlife, not to mention some much-added beauty to any yard (there's a reason they're swiftly replacing magnolias for a lot of people!).

Junipers offer dense year-round cover and provide berry-like cones that are eaten by birds and small mammals. And purple coneflowers (Echinacea purpurea) attract pollinators and produce seedheads that feed birds and other critters, too.

Of course, planting is a brilliant starting point, but there are a few other things you can do to make your garden more appealing to these fascinating birds. As ever, try leaving some areas of longer grass and natural vegetation rather than tidying every corner. Keep mature trees where possible, particularly those with natural cavities, and consider whether a species-appropriate owl nest box might be suitable for your local area.

It's also important to avoid rodent poisons, as owls can be exposed to rodenticides when they eat poisoned mice and rats. Keep lights to a minimum after the sun goes down, as they can confuse nocturnal birds and critters. And, finally, remember that owls need peace and quiet; take care to avoid disturbing potential roosting and nesting areas, particularly during breeding season.

You might not spot an owl straight away, but by planting for wildlife and allowing your garden to become a little more natural, you'll be creating a much richer habitat for all sorts of visitors. And who knows? Before too long, your owl hangout might just be the most buzzing nightspot in town...

Kayleigh is an enthusiastic (sometimes too enthusiastic!) gardener and has worked in media for over a decade. She previously served as digital editor at Stylist magazine, and has written extensively for Ideal Home, Woman & Home, Homes & Gardens, and a handful of other titles. Kayleigh is passionate about wildlife-friendly gardening, and recently cancelled her weekend plans to build a mini pond when her toddler found a frog living in their water barrel. As such, her garden – designed around the stunning magnolia tree at its centre – is filled to the brim with pollinator-friendly blooms, homemade bird feeders, and old logs for insects to nest in.

The city is making its first major investment through the Community Benefit Ordinance, using a portion of the tax generated by SpaceX AI's Colossus 1 facility for the benefit of residents.

When you purchase through links on our site, we may earn an affiliate commission. Here’s how it works.

The City of Memphis has announced that it will make its first major investment as part of its new Community Benefits Ordinance. The fund will use revenue generated by taxing SpaceX AI's Colossus 1 facility to fund various initiatives within the South Memphis neighborhoods around that data center. The fund will help with the costs associated with housing, neighborhood improvements, and services, with the first $2 million allocated for the Memphis HOPE Initiative — a new home repair assistance program that helps residents make vital repairs as well as improvements that will make their homes more accessible.

The HOPE Initiative will provide low- and moderate-income homeowners and even renters with up to $25,000 for qualifying repairs and improvements. Eligible improvements include structural repairs as well as plumbing, heating, and air conditioning repairs. Roof repairs and replacement, accessibility improvements, and some exterior improvements like painting, fencing, and landscaping are also eligible. Funds will be provided as a deferred, interest-free, forgivable loan with zero monthly payments. Any required lien will be recorded after repairs are completed and forgiven after three years.

SpaceX AI's Colossus 1 data center became fully operational in May 2025, packing 220,000 GPUs and 300 megawatts of compute capacity. A year later, in May 2026, it was announced that Anthropic had signed a lease that would see it take over the entire facility in an effort to ease usage bottlenecks across Claude's paid-for ecosystem. The company said that the additional compute afforded by Colossus 1 would enable higher Claude Code limits, the removal of throttling for Pro and Max customers, and increased API request limits for Claude Opus models. But none of that will matter to those benefiting from the Community Benefits Ordinance, of course.

The full breakdown of the $3,259,282 allocation includes:

Today's news comes hot on the heels of SpaceX AI CEO Elon Musk telling employees that the company will increase the power capacity of its data centers by 7x to 10 gigawatts by late 2027. The move would see those data centers rake in between $300 billion and $500 billion per year, according to Musk. Perhaps notably, the claim also comes as SpaceX's market capitalization dropped by nearly $570 billion in less than two months.

House Democrats Are Officially Coming for Trump’s Orbit

📅 Nov 18, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Donald Trump Jr. was barefoot and beaming when a photographer snapped a group picture of him, his new bride, and his 30-or-so wedding party guests on a private island in the Bahamas last May. But that party—which was reportedly paid for by a Russian oligarch—has since turned into a legal and political headache.

In a letter sent Wednesday, the incoming head of the House Oversight Committee has directed the president’s son to preserve all records related to his “lavish wedding celebrations” and his many business ventures, too.

That demand is a key step in any serious Congressional probe, and one sign among many that Democratic investigators are going after the president’s family in the new term following their massive wins in the election earlier this month.

Representative Robert Garcia, the incoming head of the House Oversight Committee, also issued similar record-preservation requests to Eric Trump along with Donald Trump Jr., as cofounders of the wildly controversial crypto firm World Liberty Financial, and their brother-in-law Jared Kushner, who runs a Saudi-backed investment firm while serving as a presidential envoy to the Middle East. Garcia even sent a notice to Donald Trump Jr.’s onetime fiancée Kimberly Guilfoyle, who’s been involved in a pay-for-access scandal of her own.

Garcia also sent record-retention letters to White House chief of staff Stephen Miller, for his role in directing the immigration raids that have killed at least six people and detained more than 500 US citizens; Trust for the National Mall CEO Catherine Townsend, for the $600 million in private and public money spent on the White House ballroom; and vice president JD Vance and attorney general Todd Blanche, for their roles, the letters state, in the alleged cover-up of the Epstein files. Other figures from the last year and a half of the Trump administration were listed, including former Department of Homeland Security chief Kristi Noem, her top aide Corey Lewandowski, and recently departed White House chief of staff Susie Wiles.

“As the President has said, he's willing to work with anyone to continue delivering victories for hardworking Americans. If Democrats choose to pursue sham investigations and radical left policies, they are choosing to work against the American people,” White House spokeswoman Lauren Bis tells WIRED. A representative for the Trump family did not immediately respond to a request for comment for this story.

After they won control of the House and Senate in the midterm elections, Democrats made it plain that they were going to be beyond aggressive in ramping up their investigations. (“They are going to need to plant new forests for all of the subpoenas we'll be printing,” Senator Ed Markey quipped.) The target set for those probes is beginning to come into focus more clearly, and the Democrats appear to be starting with the Trump family, who have made a killing in the president’s second term.

Now that they have the added resources and authority that come with being in the majority, Garcia and other Democrats want to dig into that growing fortune and who contributed to it.

Senator Richard Blumenthal, the ranking member of the Permanent Subcommittee on Investigations, has been looking into World Liberty and other Trump family crypto projects “for 18 months,” as a recent letter to the firm noted. Senator Elizabeth Warren has repeatedly called for investigations into World Liberty’s “national security risks,” including sales of its token to alleged North Korean hackers and Russian money launderers. Representative Ro Khanna wants to know more about the secret sale of 49 percent of World Liberty to funds controlled by Sheikh Tahnoun bin Zayed al Nahyan, the United Arab Emirates’ intelligence chief, and whether that sale had anything to do with the Trump administration’s decision to allow the sale of advanced AI chips to Abu Dhabi.

World Liberty has been particularly lucrative for the Trumps; according to Forbes, it added at one point more than $300 million to the president’s accounts. “It’s troubling that Congress might baselessly target a compliant company that has dedicated itself to expanding access to the US dollar when there are a plethora of issues afflicting the American public that need to be addressed,” World Liberty spokesperson David Wachsman tells WIRED.

World Liberty is only one family business among many that has gone through a boom cycle in the past few years. Donald Trump Jr.’s venture capital firm, 1789, went from a few hundred million under management to somewhere around $4 billion. The Trump Organization has real estate development deals underway from Bucharest to Bali.

The sudden influx of cash, Garcia notes in his letters, “raises serious concerns” that the deals are essentially “contributions to President Trump and his family in exchange for government action on behalf of the wealthy and well connected. Access to preferential treatment, federal secrets, regulatory law enforcement leniency, and the president and senior officials appears to trace back to suspicious activity associated with these schemes.”

The Trumps and their representatives have repeatedly insisted they’ve done nothing to merit this kind of scrutiny, other than have the wrong last name. “We've been dealing with the conflict-of-interest stuff for years,” Donald Trump Jr. told CNBC at a World Liberty Finance event at Mar-a-Lago in February. “Frankly, it's gotten old.”

The Russian oligarch who paid for Donald Trump Jr.’s wedding party later claimed the president’s son reimbursed him. “There is no evidence that any member of the Trump family ever sought to influence government policy or sought preferential treatment or government intervention to advance their personal or business interests,” Alan Garten, the Trump Organization’s chief legal officer, told this reporter prior to the election.

The pushback from Trumpworld has grown more intense since the Democrats’ midterm sweep. Aaron Reitz, the US attorney for the Southern District of Texas and a MAGA die-hard, sent a document-preservation letter of his own to Warren, accusing her of “terrorizing” Big Tech executives with her promises of investigation.

The White House even claimed that overly aggressive oversight might itself be a crime. “The Administration,” counsel to the president Will Scharf wrote to Garcia after the election, “will not be extorted.”

Lovable Wants You to Vibe Code Your Dream Job

📅 Nov 18, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

I squint my eyes in confusion when Anton Osika asks if I like cakes. The CEO of Lovable was in the middle of explaining how his AI startup, flush with investment cash and recently valued at over $13 billion, is ready to shift its focus after two years. Why does the vibe-coding king care if I like sweet treats? He thinks that if I do, I might be able to vibe code a living out of that.

The generative AI revolution completely flipped coding. Rather than a niche skill set where humans had to learn how to talk like computers, software development could be done by computers generating code in response to natural-language requests from humans.

Osika was early to this shift, launching a viral open-source tool in 2023, a precursor to Lovable. His mission? “Reduce barriers to build.” That initial spark ignited what is now one of Europe’s most dynamic startups.

Tech insiders talked nonstop last year about the transformative power of “vibe coding” that could empower anyone to build their own software. Parents could whip up custom apps to help track demanding club sports schedules. Picky eaters could create dynamic meal plans that adapt to whatever’s in their fridge. Polyamorous people could build software to manage their relationship hierarchies. Whatever random, niche software you can imagine, you can build it.

But Osika isn’t satisfied with helping millions of people vibe code apps for their everyday life—he also wants Lovable users to make more cash. The startup claims that around 80 percent of users arrive at the platform with a financial incentive, like building customer-facing platforms for online sales. That said, Lovable also reports that only 35 percent currently earn revenue with their projects.

That brings us back to the cake question. Lovable’s plan for next year is to help you run a small business based on your passions, like opening a cake shop. Fabian Hedin, Lovable’s other cofounder and chief technology officer, says the startup is building automations to help with everything from initial business strategy and website creation to long-term customer retention and marketing plans.

For example, a cake shop owner receives an AI-generated morning briefing through a chatbot-style, Lovable dashboard. “You set some goals, and then the AI is proactive in helping you reach those goals,” Hedin says. This briefing analyzes the number of cake boxes sold through a holiday promotion, customer emails it automatically answered, and reminders to make financial deposits on time.

Lovable has been a boon for savvy social media content creators, who can leverage their platforms better through vibe-coded sites. “People who have audiences themselves are building larger businesses around that,” Osika says. He recently went to a dinner in New York with creators, one of whom uses Lovable to help sell their book. Others have used it to help hawk merch.

Lovable earning the trust of burgeoning entrepreneurs is critical to the success of this new endeavor, though it may be an uphill battle. Vibe-coded projects were notorious for not necessarily being secure and sometimes exposing user data.

The startup was one of many vibe-coded services that came under fire this year for facilitating user-generated apps that exposed data on the open web. Earlier this month, Lovable revamped its security approach as part of its effort to make sites generated through its platform more secure by default.

“The moment real people use what you build, security is the product,” Igor Andriushchenko, Lovable’s head of security, tells WIRED. “That's why it's one of the areas we continue investing in the most and why we’ve built it directly into the platform experience.”

Osika will see Lovable as successful next year if he can help a new wave of entrepreneurs start sustainable businesses. Why just vibe code an app when you can vibe code a living?

ElevenLabs re-voiced 108 tutorial clips and we added 39 for French

📅 Nov 18, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Posted on Nov 18 • Originally published at neuragrowth.co

On 2026-11-07 we shipped a change to the Skat game that moves its tutorial narration to ElevenLabs. The 108 ordinary lesson clips, in Polish, German and English, were recorded again with the same voices used at the table. A further 39 clips, marked -fr, were recorded for the French card pattern.

Spoken text needed its own wording, so the lesson text is written differently for reading aloud. The "=" sign is either spoken as a word or replaced by a pause. Numbers are spelled out in inflected form, and 120 is written out in words in German.

The check is a script, tools/check-tutorial-voices.mjs. It sends every clip to Gemini for a transcript, then compares the words and the pace against the source text. Across all 147 clips it flags 0 outliers after the fixes. The record does not say how many it flagged before them.

The generator gained two switches. --jobs produces the list of texts to listen to, and --files re-records only the clips you name. A new test, tutorial-french, checks that the keys exist in the lessons, that DE, EN and SL translations are present, and that every step read aloud in French has a -fr recording.

The commit touches 150 files, adding 172 lines and removing 3.

When a synthetic voice reads your text, rewrite symbols and numbers as spoken words first, then transcribe every clip back and compare it to the source instead of trusting your ears.

Originally published at neuragrowth.co. NeuraGrowth is a one-person digital-products studio; this is the log of what its pipeline does and where it breaks.

The Claim Row Stopped the Duplicate. My Agent Sent the Payment Twice Anyway.

📅 Nov 18, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Yesterday I fixed my webhook duplicates with one claimable row per delivery key. A slow receiver took 2.5 seconds to answer, my sweeper ticked every second, and the same payment went out three times before the first acknowledgement landed. One row in a table, claimed atomically, and three deliveries became one. I wrote it up feeling pleased with myself. Then rudratosh left a comment that undid the whole feeling: the claim row stops the resend, but the agent still believes the step failed. Fix it quietly and the agent plans its next move from a world where no key was ever assigned, when a key exists.

He was right, and I ran it twice to find out how right. Same setup as yesterday, same slow receiver, same claim row, same key, evt-1042. The only difference between the two runs was what the planner was allowed to know.

Here is the shape of the problem. The planner watching the delivery has the patience of one second. One second after the send starts it checks for an acknowledgement, hears nothing, the receiver needs two and a half seconds, and records the step as UNKNOWN. The acknowledgement lands at about 2.5 seconds, the claim row moves to done, and the sender writes "ACK received" in its own log. All of that is true in both runs. The question is only whether the planner ever learns it before deciding what to do next.

My claim is simple, and I expect some of you to push back on it. Stopping the duplicate is the easy half of this fix. The other half is telling the planner the step succeeded, out loud, in a place it actually looks. A fix the planner never hears about is a fix that only works until the planner's next decision.

In the first run, the planner reads the claim row before planning anything. It asks for the status of evt-1042 and gets back done. The late discovery arrives as a state change it can see: UNKNOWN to SUCCEEDED, on that key, at half past three seconds into the run. Its next plan is step two, send the customer confirmation for evt-1042, and do not send evt-1042 again. The receiver logged one delivery. Zero duplicate payments.

In the second run, the planner reads nothing. The sender's log line exists, the claim row says done, but the planner never opens either one. Its belief is still UNKNOWN, the same belief it formed at one second, and it plans from that: evt-1042 must have failed, so do the step again, with a new key, evt-1043, and send the payment again. And it does. The receiver logged two deliveries, and the second one is a duplicate payment under a fresh key. Same fix, same receiver, same timings. The only difference is one read.

I ran the whole thing twice. Both runs gave the same numbers and the same plans, to the second.

The planner did nothing wrong at one second. With the information it had, UNKNOWN was the honest answer: no acknowledgement, receiver slow, step might have failed. The mistake is not the belief. The mistake is that nothing ever revisits it. A belief formed in the first second of a run is still steering the plan at the fourth second, long after the world changed underneath it, unless something goes back and checks.

This is the part people miss when they talk about retries. They argue about timeouts and intervals, the same argument as yesterday, and they treat the planner as if it watches the whole run the way a person would. It does not. It watches the moment it last looked, and then it plans. If the last look was at one second, the plan is built on a one-second-old world.

This is the finding that stung most, because the second run had the information. The sender wrote "ACK received for delivery_id=evt-1042" in its own log. The claim row sat there saying done. Both facts existed before the planner decided. Neither one mattered, because the planner never looked at either of them.

A log line is a diary entry. A state change the planner reads before deciding is feedback. The difference is not where the fact lives, it is whether the decision reads it. Every system I have built has a log full of facts nobody ever read again, and I have caught myself pointing at those logs as if their existence proved something was communicated. It proves something was written down. Those are not the same thing.

Here is the part that makes the quiet fix actively dangerous rather than merely incomplete. When the planner redoes the step, it does not reuse evt-1042. It assigns a new key, evt-1043, because from its point of view the first key belongs to a failed step. The claim row protects a key. It cannot protect a payment the planner believes was never made. The duplicate in my second run did not slip past the claim row. It walked around it, through the front door, wearing a new key.

That is why "fix it quietly when nothing downstream depends on the agent knowing" is a narrower rule than it sounds. The payment confirmation, the next step in the chain, the customer email, all of them depend on the agent knowing. The only step where the quiet fix is safe is one with no next step at all, and those are rarer than they look.

The fix for this half is embarrassingly small, which is probably why it gets skipped. Before the planner decides the next step, it reads the claim row for the key it is deciding about. One read, one status, done or not. In my run that read is the entire difference between one delivery and two, between a confirmation email and a duplicate payment.

There is a temptation to make this fancier, to build a whole notification channel, an event bus, a watcher. Resist it, at least at first. The planner already knows how to read the row. The row already holds the truth. The missing piece was never a channel. It was the habit of reading before deciding.

Two honest limits, because the test was small. First, this was a tabletop model of a planning loop on my own machine, not a real agent framework: one key, one receiver, a planner with exactly one rule, plan from the freshest state you actually read. A real agent has more inputs, but the failure is the same shape, a belief formed early and never corrected. Second, the claim row still needs a lease in real life, a crashed claimer holds its row forever, and that part is still untested here, same as yesterday.

If you run agents against webhooks, or against anything where a step can succeed slowly, you have met this bug. What did your agent do with the late success, redo the work, or carry on as if nothing had happened? And where does your planner read the state back, before it plans, or never?

P.S. Everything I write stays free — that part doesn't change. But if this one was worth something to you, you can buy me a coffee ☕ (one-time or monthly, both work). It covers the API credits and hosting behind these experiments — and honestly, it's also the nudge that gets the next one started on the evenings I'd otherwise skip it. — Salam

How We Built a Receive-Only Email Honeypot with Postfix

📅 Nov 18, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

We set up a small lab to collect fresh samples of unsolicited email for security research. An Ubuntu 24.04 LTS cloud server runs Postfix and saves incoming messages to Maildir. The project notes are hosted separately on GitHub Pages.

This English version was translated and edited with AI assistance from our original deployment notes.

We set the MX record for the recipient domain trap.quietpostlab.me to point to mx.trap.quietpostlab.me with a priority of 10. An A record for that host points to the server’s public IPv4 address.

A sending mail server resolves the MX host and connects over TCP port 25. We checked the cloud firewall rule and the local Postfix listener, then tested the connection from outside the server’s network.

Postfix treats the recipient subdomain as a virtual mailbox domain. The configured addresses on that domain map to a Maildir owned by a dedicated system account. Each delivery creates a file that preserves the message headers, body, and MIME attachments.

We restricted acceptance to the lab domain, rejected third-party relay requests, and disabled SMTP authentication and outbound delivery. We set a 20 MiB message limit, bounded connection counts and timeouts, and enabled log rotation. Postfix defers incoming mail when the queue filesystem runs below its free-space threshold.

These are the three checks we used on the server.

We sent two marked test messages from outside the server, one to its public IP and one to the MX hostname. Both arrived in Maildir. We checked the Chinese subject line, plain text, HTML, and attachment. An archived message was still readable after restarting Postfix. The server also rejected test relay requests for recipients outside the lab domain.

Some anonymous requests to our original web host returned HTTP 403, so we moved the project notes to GitHub Pages. The web pages and the SMTP service use separate DNS names. We assign a distinct address to each publication and use the actual delivery recipient to measure time to first confirmed spam and count new message patterns after deduplication.

Mail sent to this experimental address may be archived for email security research. Please keep personal and confidential information out of your message.

Building WhatsApp AI agents with Amazon Bedrock

📅 Nov 18, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Posted on Nov 18 • Edited on Nov 18 • Originally published at agentic-affairs.com

AWS just handed developers a shortcut to putting an agent on WhatsApp — and then, in the fine print, reminded everyone it's a shortcut, not a finished product. The toolkit, SMART, wires up the messy channel plumbing around Bedrock AgentCore. Useful? Genuinely. Production-ready out of the box? AWS itself says no.

The agent loop is the easy part. The hard part is everything WhatsApp demands around it: nested payloads that change shape by message type, voice notes through Transcribe, read receipts, typing indicators, rate limits, and Meta's 24-hour rule — message after a day of silence and you're stuck with a pre-approved template. SMART packages all of that into an SDK and a Terraform stack so teams can spend their time on prompts and business logic instead of webhook archaeology.

The pitch is uv run socialagent deploy and you're live. The reality: you still need a linked WhatsApp Business Account, a registered number, a region with AgentCore available, model access — and then a manual bind step pointing your account's event destination at the stack's SNS topic. Skip that and inbound messages go nowhere, silently. A successful Terraform run is not a working conversation.

AgentCore memory persists conversation history across sessions, scoped per phone number — but the documented default retention is seven days. That's a design constraint, not a footnote: if your users resume conversations after a longer gap, the "memory" you sold them has already expired.

The walkthrough cites Webmotors cutting a two-month engagement to six weeks. That's AWS reporting its own customer's timeline — not an independent benchmark, and not a delivery estimate for your team. Treat it as a case study, not a promise.

SMART provisions a Bedrock Guardrails resource by default and wires up signature verification, API keys, and Secrets Manager. What it doesn't do is decide whether your safeguards are actually right. AWS leaves that call to the team deploying — which is honest, and exactly the part most teams will skip.

The full walkthrough — architecture, the three blueprints, the local dev loop, and the template-sync trap that can fail a deploy — is on Agentic Affairs:

Building WhatsApp AI agents with Amazon Bedrock

The Polestar 4 Is Getting Vehicle-To-Load, But The U.S. And Canada Are Left Out

📅 Nov 18, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Browse this and many other websites without banner ads, personalized tracking and video ads.

The Polestar 4 electric crossover is getting several new features through an over-the-air software update. The Chinese-owned, Swedish-based automaker said the latest P4.3.0 version will be available for all Polestar 4s on the road, but the feature list will vary depending on where the cars were sold.

The biggest news is the addition of vehicle-to-load (V2L), which allows owners to export power from the car’s high-voltage battery through a dedicated adapter that plugs into the charging port. The Polestar 4 can provide up to 3.3 kilowatts of power for camping equipment, tools, appliances, and other electronic devices.

That being said, the feature will not be available in the United States, Canada, or South Korea. The Polestar 4 has been the company’s best-selling model stateside ever since the Polestar 2 was discontinued, and leaving this feature out is a huge downer for American and Canadian owners.

But not all hope is lost. The latest software version comes with other goodies, which will be available globally, including a Camping Mode that keeps the air conditioning system and some USB outlets active when the car is switched off. Together with V2L, it makes the Polestar 4 a true off-grid camping machine, as long as you live in Europe.

2026- and 2027-model-year cars that were spec’d with the Pilot Pack will be able to automatically steer and brake into perpendicular parking spaces after updating to the latest software version. Meanwhile, the newly added Valet Mode can put the owner’s mind at ease when handing the car to a stranger, with restricted vehicle access, configurable speed limits, and glovebox locking.

Drivers who use compatible Android smartphones from Google or Samsung can use their devices as digital keys. Customers can lock, unlock, and start their vehicle using their smartphone. The addition of the digital key feature for Android devices comes after Polestar introduced it for Apple iOS users last year.

On top of it all comes Android Automotive OS 15, a newer version of the operating system designed specifically for cars. Polestar says the infotainment system is now more responsive and the car starts up faster than before.

“Over-the-air updates allow the Polestar 4 to continue to evolve long after delivery, and this latest update brings some of the most widely requested customer features,” said Johanna Bergh, Head of Product Management at Polestar. “From V2L and Camping mode to digital key and Android 15, all Polestar 4 vehicles become even more capable, versatile and connected through software alone.”

Polestar is now on its last legs in the United States, after it was effectively banned from selling cars here from the 2027 model year onward. The company still has a handful of Polestar and Polestar 4 models in stock, and it has some extremely attractive offers to try to empty lots by the year’s end. The Polestar 4, for example, has a $25,000 incentive for cash purchases and a $19,000 incentive for leases. The same $25,000 cash bonus also applies to the larger Polestar 3.

Not Micron. Not Broadcom. This Artificial Intelligence (AI) Stock Will Skyrocket in December After the Latest Sell-Off

📅 Nov 18, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Optical networking is emerging as the next bottleneck in AI infrastructure, and Ciena is one of the best ways for investors to capitalize on this market's growth.

The recent sell-off in the semiconductor sector has weighed heavily on shares of Micron Technology and Broadcom.

While Micron has lost 26% of its value over the past three months, Broadcom stock has dropped 15%. The PHLX Semiconductor Sector index, for comparison, is down 23% over the same period. Both companies will be looking to win back investor confidence when they release their quarterly results in December.

Broadcom will release its fiscal 2026 results on Dec. 9, while Micron is expected to report results later next month. I won't be surprised to see both semiconductor stocks deliver stronger-than-expected results and provide healthy guidance, as they are benefiting from bottlenecks in the artificial intelligence (AI) infrastructure space.

However, there is another AI infrastructure company -- Ciena (CIEN +2.82%) -- which will release its quarterly results on Dec. 10, and its shares could get a nice shot in the arm following its report. Let's see why that's likely to be the case.

It is well known that a scarce memory supply has been a tailwind for Micron. Meanwhile, Broadcom has benefited from the shift to custom AI processors in data centers and from the need for fast networking chips. The demand for Broadcom's chips has been exceeding supply, driving solid growth in its revenue and earnings.

Similarly, Ciena has been delivering phenomenal growth on the back of strong demand for optical networking components in AI data centers. The company expects $1.75 billion in revenue in the fourth quarter of fiscal 2026, implying a 30% increase over the prior year. Even better, it predicts non-GAAP operating margin climbing to 20% from 13.2% in the year-ago period. As a result, analysts are forecasting a 128% increase in Ciena's earnings per share for fiscal Q4.

The substantial uptick in Ciena's margins is attributable to "an extraordinary industry demand environment" that's exceeding supply. Healthy demand and the accompanying supply constraint are pushing up prices for Ciena's optical networking components. Importantly, Ciena expects strong demand for optical networking to drive up its margins over the next three years.

At its investor forum held in September this year, the company pointed out that its addressable market will increase at a 24% compound annual growth rate (CAGR) between 2026 and 2029, reaching $52 billion after three years. Ciena, meanwhile, is predicting a 30% revenue CAGR over this period, suggesting it is on track to outpace the broader market's growth.

More importantly, the company estimates that its operating margin will jump to a 25%-27% range in fiscal 2027 and then increase to 32%-35% in 2029. For comparison, Ciena's operating margin was 22.5% in the third quarter of fiscal 2026. However, I won't be surprised if Ciena's growth exceeds its own expectations, as demand for optical networking is poised to take off.

Ciena is receiving far more orders than it can fulfill, as evidenced by its book-to-bill ratio of "significantly greater than 1." In fact, management noted on Ciena's September earnings call that its order inflow in the first month of fiscal Q4 was approaching the orders it booked in entirety of Q3.

As a result, Ciena expects to end fiscal 2026 with more than $10 billion in backlog. The company anticipates its top line to increase by more than 30% in fiscal 2027 to $8.3 billion to $8.4 billion. However, it could do better than that as the optical networking market's revenue could jump by more than 10x between 2026 and 2028, according to Goldman Sachs, reaching $154 billion in 2028.

Ciena stock has lost almost 40% of its value over the past three months. It trades at 25 times forward earnings, which is quite attractive for a company whose earnings per share (EPS) are predicted to jump substantially over the long run.

CIEN EPS LT Growth Estimates data by YCharts

Ciena is expected to deliver $7.17 in EPS for the recently concluded fiscal 2026. Its bottom line could more than double over the next two years.

CIEN EPS Estimates for Current Fiscal Year data by YCharts

Assuming Ciena trades at 30 times earnings at the end of fiscal 2028, its stock price could reach $526 in two years (based on the $17.54 EPS estimate). That implies a 104% jump, which is why investors should consider buying this AI stock following its recent slide, before it makes a parabolic move after it releases results next month.

Stock Market Correction: 2 AI Stocks With 130% and 160% Upside, According to Wall Street.

📅 Nov 18, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Historically, Nasdaq market corrections have been a great time to buy stocks.

The Nasdaq Composite (^IXIC -0.74%) entered correction territory earlier this month amid concerns about the sustainability of the artificial intelligence boom. But history says the sell-off is a buying opportunity. In the last decade, the index returned an average of 21% during the year following its first close in correction territory.

Wall Street analysts see substantial upside in the following AI stocks:

Sandisk manufactures storage devices based on NAND flash memory. Historically, the company has focused on consumer products such as SD cards, USB flash drives, and portable solid-state drives (SSDs). But Sandisk is increasingly targeting the enterprise SSD market to capitalize on growing demand for artificial intelligence infrastructure.

Sandisk is smaller than competitors Samsung and SK Hynix, but its joint venture with Japanese manufacturer Kioxia offers supply chain security and cost advantages. The companies share investments in semiconductor fabrication equipment, allowing Sandisk to purchase NAND flash memory wafers at cost plus a small markup rather than pay market prices.

"NAND flash is emerging as the only economically viable solution to deliver the capacity, performance, and efficiency required to keep models accessible for real-time inference at scale," said CEO David Goeckeler. "This shift in understanding the critical nature of our technology comes at a time when our product differentiation is strongest."

Sandisk reported strong financial results in the first quarter of fiscal 2027, which ended in October. Revenue rose 378% to $11 billion, driven primarily by quadruple-digit sales growth in the data center segment. Sandisk also reported triple-digit sales growth in the edge segment, which supplies flash storage for industrial equipment and consumer devices like laptops and gaming consoles.

However, Sandisk is currently reaping the benefits of a memory chip supply shortage fueled by intense demand for AI infrastructure. NAND prices are projected to quadruple this year due to that shortage. But the memory industry is highly cyclical. A supply glut could develop by 2029 as manufacturers expand production capacity, driving prices (and profits) lower.

Sandisk trades at 6.5 times earnings. That seems cheap for a company whose earnings are forecast to grow at 40% annually over the next three years. But earnings could drop sharply during the next industry downturn, and uncertainty concerning the timing and severity of that downturn make Sandisk a risky bet.

CoreWeave operates a cloud computing platform purpose-built for artificial intelligence. While general-purpose clouds like Amazon and Microsoft support a broad range of tasks, CoreWeave rents access to large clusters of powerful GPUs that are primarily used for a single purpose: training and running AI models.

CoreWeave's competitive edge comes from its specialized infrastructure and expertise in managing large GPU clusters. The company routinely achieves leading results at the MLPerf benchmarks, objective tests that evaluate AI systems across training and inference tasks. In September, independent research company SemiAnalysis recognized CoreWeave as the best AI cloud for the third consecutive time.

"CoreWeave continues to set the benchmark for AI cloud performance by demonstrating strong technical execution and operational maturity in managing large-scale AI solutions," wrote Dylan Patel, chief analyst at SemiAnalysis.

CoreWeave reported solid third-quarter financial results, sending shares 25% higher the next day. Revenue rose 168% to $3.6 billion, up from 112% in the prior quarter. Contracted revenue backlog soared 138% to $132 billion. But debt-fueled expansion is still a concern. Interest expense consumed 23% of revenue, and non-GAAP loss widened to $529 million.

CoreWeave trades at 2.7 times sales. That looks cheap when Wall Street expects revenue to increase at 96% annually through 2028. That means CoreWeave currently trades at 0.6 times projected 2028 sales. Of course, the company must still prove its debt-fueled growth can generate sustainable profits, and rising interest rates will make profitability harder to reach. But the stock could deliver substantial upside if CoreWeave executes.

Al 15 novembre 2026, Bitmine deteneva complessivamente 5.227.037 ETH in staking, per un valore di 9,5 miliardi di dollari calcolato a 1.823 dollari per ETH. Nota cautelativa sulle dichiarazioni previsionali

Il presente comunicato stampa contiene dichiarazioni che costituiscono "dichiarazioni previsionali", ai sensi del Private Securities Litigation Reform Act del 1995 e successive modifiche. Le dichiarazioni previsionali comprendono tutte le dichiarazioni che non hanno natura esclusivamente storica e possono essere generalmente identificabili dall'utilizzo di termini quali "si attende", "proietta", "intende", "pianifica", "crede", "si aspetta", "stima", "puÃ²", "potrÃ ", "potrebbe", "dovrebbe", "sembra", "ritiene" o espressioni analoghe. In particolare, il presente comunicato stampa contiene dichiarazioni previsionali riguardanti: (i) l'obiettivo della SocietÃ  di acquisizione del 5% dell'offerta totale di ETH (iniziativa "Alchemy of 5%"); (ii) la strategia della SocietÃ  relativa all'accumulo e alla gestione della tesoreria degli asset digitali, comprese le dichiarazioni riguardanti il proseguimento degli acquisti settimanali di ETH e la posizione della SocietÃ  quale maggiore tesoreria ETH al mondo; (iii) le attivitÃ  di staking della SocietÃ , compresi premi da staking ETH annualizzati e previsti pari a circa 285 milioni di dollari a regime e ricavi da staking annualizzati pari a circa 245 milioni di dollari, sulla base degli ETH attualmente in staking; (iv) l'espansione di MAVAN al servizio di investitori istituzionali, depositari e partner dell'ecosistema; (v) le valutazioni del management sulle condizioni del mercato delle criptovalute, compresa l'opinione secondo cui Ã¨ iniziata una corsa al rialzo delle criptovalute, le opinioni concernenti le cause delle recenti flessioni dei prezzi di ETH, tra cui il potenziale impatto degli sviluppi relativi a Nvidia, la vendita a margine, e la tempistica e la portata previste dell'offerta pubblica iniziale di Anthropic, e l'opinione secondo cui l'andamento recente del prezzo di ETH potrebbe rispecchiare l'andamento del prezzo di ETH precedente all'offerta pubblica iniziale di SpaceX; (vi) l'opinione del management in merito al potenziale impatto del GENIUS Act e del SEC Project Crypto sui servizi finanziari; e (vii) le dichiarazioni relative al valore delle partecipazioni in criptovalute, liquiditÃ , titoli negoziabili e investimenti "moonshot" della SocietÃ .

Tali dichiarazioni previsionali comportano rischi e incertezze significativi che potrebbero determinare risultati effettivi sostanzialmente diversi da quelli espressi o implicitamente indicati. I fattori che potrebbero determinare o contribuire a tali differenze comprendono, a titolo esemplificativo e non esaustivo: l'estrema volatilitÃ  e imprevedibilitÃ  dei prezzi degli asset digitali, inclusi ETH e Bitcoin; la dipendenza della SocietÃ  da fonti terze per la determinazione dei prezzi e dai valori di mercato comunicati, che possono subire variazioni significative; i cambiamenti nelle condizioni di mercato che potrebbero incidere sul prezzo e sui volumi di negoziazione dei titoli della SocietÃ ; la capacitÃ  della SocietÃ  di attuare con successo la propria strategia di acquisizione di asset digitali e di raggiungere gli obiettivi di accumulo di ETH, incluso l'obiettivo "Alchemy of 5%"; la capacitÃ  della SocietÃ  di finanziare le proprie attivitÃ  operative e l'espansione di MAVAN; i rischi operativi, di sicurezza e tecnologici connessi alle attivitÃ  di staking e validazione della SocietÃ , inclusi malfunzionamenti della rete, eventi di slashing, violazioni della sicurezza informatica, compresi furti e attacchi informatici ai danni di exchange, depositari e altri operatori del settore, come il recente furto ai danni di Binance, nonchÃ© modifiche ai protocolli; il rischio che i rendimenti, i premi e i ricavi da staking effettivi differiscano in misura significativa dagli importi previsti; la concorrenza nei settori della gestione di tesoreria degli asset digitali, dello staking e del mining; la dipendenza della SocietÃ  da personale chiave; gli sviluppi normativi riguardanti gli asset digitali, la tecnologia blockchain e le attivitÃ  di staking, inclusi l'eventuale approvazione definitiva, l'attuazione e l'interpretazione del GENIUS Act e di altre proposte legislative pendenti; i provvedimenti adottati dalla SEC, dalla CFTC e da altri organismi di regolamentazione; i rischi connessi agli investimenti "moonshot" della SocietÃ , inclusi quelli in Eightco Holdings e Beast Industries; i fattori macroeconomici, tra cui inflazione, tassi di interesse e condizioni economiche generali; le modifiche al protocollo Ethereum; le prestazioni dei fornitori di servizi terzi, degli exchange, dei depositari e dei partner di staking; nonchÃ© gli altri fattori di rischio descritti nei documenti depositati dalla SocietÃ  presso la SEC.

Le dichiarazioni previsionali contenute nel presente comunicato stampa si basano sulle informazioni a disposizione del management alla data dal presente comunicato e riflettono le attuali aspettative, stime e convinzioni del management in merito a eventi futuri. I risultati effettivi potrebbero differire in misura significativa da quelli espressi o impliciti in base ai fattori sopra descritti e da quelli riportati nella sezione "Fattori di rischio" dell'Annual Report della SocietÃ  su Form 10-K relativo all'esercizio chiuso al 30 settembre 2025, depositato presso la SEC il 21 novembre 2025, nei Quarterly Reports della SocietÃ  su Form 10-Q e negli altri documenti depositati dalla SocietÃ  presso la SEC, come eventualmente modificati o aggiornati nel tempo. Copie di tali documenti sono disponibili sul sito www.sec.gov e all'indirizzo https://Bitminetech.io/investor-relations/. La SocietÃ  invita i lettori a non fare eccessivo affidamento sulle dichiarazioni previsionali, che riflettono esclusivamente le circostanze esistenti alla data in cui vengono formulate. Will GTA 6 be on Xbox Game Pass at launch? Everything you need to know.

📅 Nov 18, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

After a decade of waiting, GTA 6 is finally here. If you've already dropped $80 on a preorder and pre-loaded the game, then you're all set.

However, if you're hoping to play GTA 6 as part of your Xbox Game Pass subscription, then I have some bad news for you: Grand Theft Auto VI will not be included in Xbox Game Pass at launch. Unfortunately, the same goes for PC gamers. So, if you're a casual player who was hoping to avoid buying a $600 gaming console just to play GTA 6, Xbox Game Pass isn't the solution.

In an interesting twist, Xbox recently announced that GTA 6 will be available to play on Xbox Series S/X (and even older Xbox One consoles) via Xbox Cloud Gaming. Unfortunately, the graphics will be downgraded, and Xbox Cloud Gaming now has strict monthly limits, likely because of GTA 6.

Will GTA 6 be added to the Xbox Game Pass library at a later date? Probably! In fact, previous GTA games have been included in the subscription. However, even if GTA 6 is eventually added to the Xbox Game Pass, it could be a while.

Xbox Game Pass is a monthly subscription service that provides gamers with online console multiplayer access along with a library of titles. Xbox Game Pass actually includes quite a selection of games, depending on your plan. For example, the $22.99 per month Game Pass Ultimate plan includes more than 500 titles, including a selection of launch-day games, and doesn't require an Xbox console.

Not all games are added to Xbox Game Pass. Many popular titles do eventually get added, but usually not from day one. The best tech deal this week The Apple iPhone 18 Pro is free right now with T-Mobile Products available for purchase through affiliate links. If you buy something through links on our site, Mashable may earn an affiliate commission.

The Apple iPhone 18 Pro is free right now with T-Mobile

Strauss Zelnick, the CEO of GTA developer Rockstar Games' parent company Take-Two, addressed the organization's view of game subscription services during a GamesIndustry.biz live event in 2022.

"We've supported various subscription services, and we're happy to do so," Zelnick said at the time. "Our skepticism has been around making frontline console products available day and date with subscription. That doesn't make any sense to us, because economically speaking, we don't think consumers are prepared to pay for that. And we can't afford to turn our business upside down in a way that doesn't make sense economically. There always has to be an intersection between what the consumer wants and what the publisher can do. And you know, it doesn't make sense to do that for our properties."

Rockstar Games' GTA 6 predecessor, Grand Theft Auto V, has been included in the Xbox Game Pass numerous times over the years. GTA 5 was available on Game Pass in 2020, 2021, 2023, and 2025, and usually stayed on the service for a few months before being removed.

GTA 5's most recent stint on Xbox Game Pass ended on April 15, 2026.

With that said, GTA 5 was released all the way back in 2013, and its first inclusion in Xbox Game Pass was more than six and a half years later.

So, will gamers have to wait nearly seven years for GTA 6 to be added to Xbox Game Pass or other cloud gaming subscriptions? We just don't know. But one thing is certain: if you want to play GTA 6 right away, you're better off buying it to play on either an Xbox or PlayStation console.

Revealed: The unknown holding midfielder Liverpool NEED to sign in January

📅 Nov 18, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Despite Trey Nyoni's breakthrough this season, Liverpool are still light in the centre of the park

When you purchase through links on our site, we may earn an affiliate commission. Here’s how it works.

Andoni Iraola's high press is yet to take its toll on the Liverpool squad after only 10 Premier League games, but there is every chance it will as the season wears on.

While the likes of Dominik Szoboszlai, Ryan Gravenberch and Alexis Mac Allister provide excellent press resistance and ball progression, for each of them to play 50-or-more games across all competitions in such a demanding system could prove untenable.

More urgent is that when the first wave of Liverpool's press is bypassed, the lack of a defensive shield leaves the backline exposed to central counter-attacks, which most notably occurred on the opening day of the new season against Newcastle.

If Liverpool are to maintain their competitive edge in the second half of the season, identifying a specialist No. 6 in the January transfer window is advisable.

According to a new data visualisation from Ballity Insight Lab analysing 68 LaLiga midfielders across the 2026/27 season, the profile Liverpool require is currently thriving in Spain. The below model plots 'duel destroyers' against 'ball-winners' seeking to find out which midfielders are good at both functions.

La Liga’s toughest midfielders? Duel Destroyer vs Ball Winner 📊 Wins it and wins duels: 🇦🇷 Guido Rodríguez, 🇪🇸 Rodri, 🇨🇴 Deossa, 🇫🇷 Camavinga, 🇪🇸 Gorrotxategi, 🏴󠁧󠁢󠁥󠁮󠁧󠁿 Bellingham, 🇩🇰 Hjulmand, 🇪🇸 Bernal, 🇫🇷 Lemar, 🇪🇸 Barrios Formula in the comments 👇 https://t.co/6rcVRo63vUNovember 16, 2026

The top-right quadrant of this scatter plot is categorised as the 'wins it and wins duels' bracket.

This zone is reserved for midfielders who consistently break up opposition play with lots of tackling and interceptions, whilst also dominating individual physical battles.

The best features, fun and footballing quizzes, straight to your inbox every week.

Unsurprisingly, the quadrant is populated by unattainable global stars such as Real Madrid duo Eduardo Camavinga and Jude Bellingham, alongside more established anchors like Guido Rodriguez and Morten Hjulmand.

However, just beneath those household names are two statistical outliers who could be January targets for the Anfield recruitment team: Jon Gorrotxategi and Nelson Deossa.

Gorrotxategi, who like Iraola is of Basque descent and currently represents Real Sociedad, stands out as the most compelling statistical fit for Liverpool’s needs.

The Spanish midfielder is positioned near the 95th percentile in the 'duel destroyer' category while simultaneously sitting around the 88th percentile as a ball-winner.

He boasts an aggressive, front-foot defensive style that would suit the demands of Premier League football.

The data suggests Gorrotxategi could function as Liverpool's midfield backstop, sweeping up loose balls and engaging opposition ball-carriers before they can reach the Reds' defensive third.

Deossa, meanwhile, presents an intriguing, slightly more balanced alternative, which perhaps Liverpool already have covered in their midfield stable.

The Colombian midfielder ranks just below the 90th percentile for ball-winning and hovers around the 85th percentile for duels. His profile suggests he is a player who reads the game well to block passing lanes, while retaining the physical bite required to win ground duels in the middle third.

Relying on converted No. 8s, or an up-and-coming 19-year-old, to anchor a title-challenging midfield is a compromise that opponents will punish as the season deepens. Liverpool need a player capable of holding central spaces when the full-backs push high and to win second balls from clearances.

The likes of Camavinga or Hjulmand may dominate mainstream transfer gossip columns this winter, but the smart money lies in targeting the anomalies.

In going for either Gorrotxategi or Real Betis man Deossa in January, Liverpool would acquire a midfield destroyer capable of plugging the gaps and protecting the defence, ultimately freeing their more technical midfielders to focus on unlocking opposition low blocks. Neither will come cheap, though, as both players are contracted to their respective clubs until June 2030.

Pochettino saluta gli USA: "Sognavamo il Mondiale, ma non abbiamo avuto abbastanza tempo"

📅 Nov 18, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Mauricio Pochettino ha chiuso la sua esperienza da commissario tecnico degli Stati Uniti con una vittoria. Il successo per 2-0 contro Haiti ha rappresentato l'ultimo atto del suo mandato iniziato nel 2024, prima del ritorno in Premier League sulla panchina del Manchester United. Al termine della partita, l'allenatore argentino ha salutato giocatori e staff tra abbracci, sorrisi e grande emozione.

"Volevamo concludere bene. Naturalmente è difficile, perché è emozionante. Voglio davvero bene a questi ragazzi, ai giocatori e allo staff", ha dichiarato Pochettino dopo la gara. Il tecnico ha ammesso quanto sia stata complicata la decisione di lasciare la Nazionale americana, definendola una delle scelte più difficili della sua carriera. Durante il suo periodo alla guida degli Stati Uniti, Pochettino ha raggiunto gli ottavi di finale del Mondiale 2026, dove la squadra è stata eliminata dal Belgio con un netto 4-1. Nonostante il risultato finale, l'argentino ritiene di aver lasciato basi importanti.

"Credo che il nostro modo di giocare rappresenti ciò che sognavamo quando siamo arrivati. Sapevamo di avere giocatori talentuosi e abbiamo costruito principi solidi", ha spiegato. Pochettino ha poi evidenziato il percorso recente: "Nelle ultime sei partite siamo rimasti imbattuti contro avversari di valore e la squadra si è davvero divertita". Resta però un rimpianto: "Il nostro sogno era vincere il Mondiale, ma è vero che non abbiamo avuto abbastanza tempo. Sono un po' deluso". Allo stesso tempo, però, il tecnico guarda con fiducia al futuro: "Sono felice di aver trasformato un'idea nella convinzione che possiamo vincere e competere contro le migliori squadre del mondo. Questa è la strada da seguire".

PS5 Pro stock updates: Where to buy Sonys console for GTA 6 (without overpaying)

📅 Nov 18, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

You'll be hard-pressed to find a PlayStation 5 Pro in stock right now. Major retailers, including PlayStation Direct, Best Buy, Amazon (including bundle offers), Target, and Walmart, are sold out. With the upcoming release of Grand Theft Auto VI, the reseller market has gotten a little ridiculous (as has everything else).

Beyond the obvious hype for Rockstar's next release, Sony is dealing with broader supply chain bottlenecks. The global AI boom is squeezing the semiconductor market, which means Sony can't build consoles fast enough. According to distributor reports, these supply constraints could keep stock tight well into 2027.

Naturally, resellers are having a field day. We've seen third-party listings on reseller sites (and even GameStop's official pre-owned page) gouging buyers for $1,300 to $1,500+ for a console that retails for $899. Sony is taking the issue so seriously that in Japan, they're forcing buyers to enter a lottery — and prove they've clocked at least 60 hours of PS4 or PS5 playtime over the last two years — just for a chance to buy a PS5 Pro. If you absolutely have to buy one today and have money to burn, here's what the third-party market currently looks like:

Amazon: Used PS5 Pro consoles are selling for more than $1,700; no new versions are currently available.

GameStop: A pre-owned unit will currently run you $1,399.99.

Swappa: This is a legitimate, verified marketplace for used tech. However, verified PS5 Pro listings are currently sitting at $1,338.

StockX: The popular resale bidding site currently has the console hovering around $1,239.

Walmart: Don't be fooled by third-party sellers on major retail sites. A low-rated seller on Walmart is currently asking $1,669.99 (open-box units are already sold out).

Mercari: We previously found a listing here for $800. Since this is $100 below the official MSRP for a highly coveted, sold-out item, we think it was almost certainly a scam. Avoid deals that sound too good to be true.

If you're set on securing a Pro before or after launch day, here's how to play the restock game without burning a hole in your wallet:

Sign up for stock alerts. Restocks happen in small, sudden waves. Set up notifications through stock-tracking accounts on X (like @PS5StockUpdater) or apps like HotStock to get pinged the second inventory hits.

Bookmark and refresh product pages. Keep direct product pages saved for PlayStation Direct, Best Buy, Amazon, Target, and Walmart. PlayStation Direct in particular occasionally drops invite-only or queue-based stock.

Check local inventory. Sometimes brick-and-mortar stores (like local GameStops or Target stores) receive small physical allocations that don't always show up online right away. It never hurts to ask an associate in person.

Preorder the game now. Don't hold your breath for a last-minute GTA 6 PS5 Pro console bundle. Secure your physical or digital preorder now so you're ready to go, no matter which console sits under your TV on day one.

Palmer Luckey says his SLAB seastead is 'way cooler' than a floating data center

📅 Nov 18, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

It would act as a sovereign city state free from things like laws and governments.

Oculus founder and Oxytocin booster Palmer Luckey is touting a new project that combines some of his favorite things like wacky projects and libertarianism. SLAB is an informal plan for a floating "seastead" designed to last a thousand years that would act as a sovereign state to avoid pesky laws that "cripple" corporations, Luckey said.

An initial AI render depicts a massive artificial island built high above the sea with what looks like office buildings, apartments, refineries... and is that a data center at the top left?

"At a time when most of the seasteading community wanted to escape the US and smoke pot, this was the one project explicitly designed to make America stronger, a relief valve for ill-considered or outdated rules and regulations," Luckey wrote in a post that must be read to be believed.

You may have questions like "wtf is seasteading" and "isn't this just a floating AI data center?" That confusion is understandable, as it uses language and ideas around far-out moonshot concepts that may not be familiar to normal, non-tech-billionaires.

Selling Oculus to Facebook in 2014 gave me money for helicopter flight lessons and developing a seastead called SLAB with a 1,000 year design life.

The goal was to be cheaper than land and absent state/local laws that cripple critical American industries like metal processing,... pic.twitter.com/aJkd0eOgLF

— Palmer Luckey (@PalmerLuckey) November 18, 2026

The idea with seasteading is that if you build a floating structure far enough out in the ocean, you can create your own city-state and make whatever laws you want. The concept has been touted for years by several tech bros including Peter Thiel and Joe Lonsdale. However, the only real seastead you may have heard about was Sea Island that the piracy site Pirate Bay eyed at one point as a base of operations.

"The goal was to be cheaper than land and absent state/local laws that cripple critical American industries like metal processing, medical research/treatment, desalination, oil refining [and] chemical engineering," said Luckey. "[W]hile it still has a long way to go, there has been significant progress towards my goal of reviving manifest destiny and westward expansion."

That raises even more questions! Like, why is Luckey talking about manifest destiny? And what kind of medical research needs to be done at sea beyond the reach of laws? In any case, Luckey, who runs the defense contract firm Anduril and plans on breeding military dogs, said he wanted to get this SLAB info out now as he was being asked about it by reporters (who later identified themselves as Liz Hoffman and Reed Albergotti, both from Semafor).

Palmer said that the journalists "plan on calling SLAB my new floating AI datacenter project." However, he offered a riposte that may be the most cringe thing you'll read today: "It is way cooler than that, way older than that, and I hate looking like a hype-chasing consensus loser, so now you have to read this tweet."

Citi and JPMorgan reportedly add chip-curbs clause to Bain data centre loan

📅 Nov 18, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Bridge Data Centres once sold computing power to Megaspeed, a firm under US investigation over alleged Nvidia chip smuggling.

Citigroup and JPMorgan Chase have added terms to a potential loan for Bain Capital-owned Bridge Data Centres that make the borrower responsible for following US chip rules, Bloomberg reported. The loan could grow to $6.6bn.

The wording covers US export controls and the Commerce Department’s Entity List, people familiar with the talks told Bloomberg. The documents could still change. TNW has not independently verified the report.

The clause could let the banks call a default if a breach is found, Bloomberg reported. Its sources said the wording is more specific than the standard sanctions terms in such loans.

Singapore-based Bridge previously sold computing capacity at a data centre in Malaysia to Megaspeed International. The US is investigating whether Megaspeed smuggled Nvidia chips into China. Megaspeed has denied any wrongdoing.

Bridge replaced Megaspeed with cloud provider Zenlayer earlier this year, according to a memo it sent to lenders in February.

The banks made the changes before news broke of former Nvidia boss Jensen Huang’s relationship with Megaspeed founder Alice Huang, Bloomberg’s sources said. The decisions came from the banks’ head offices in New York.

Meanwhile, about a dozen bankers at Asian and European lenders told Bloomberg their firms had not changed their loan terms. They said US banks face the greater risk if things go wrong.

The move comes days after Washington issued rules to close a loophole that let Chinese firms rent Nvidia chips abroad. JPMorgan and Citi declined to comment to Bloomberg.
